- 07:00. RES-17 reaper, second try: worker 767 (idle since the 05:48 probe) was gone between 06:24 and 06:27, when it reached about 30 min of guest time idle (37 min of wall time less the 5.75 min the guest was frozen in hibernation); worker 1035 (last cell about 06:24) was still there at 06:51, and the computer hibernated at 06:53:00, 30.4 min after the 06:22:38 wake. So neither API-key requests nor a lifecycle start on a running computer move vmctl's idle clock (only resolveDesktopContext and the lifecycle transitions set LastActiveAt), and on an idle non-protected computer hibernation (30 min) arrives with the reaper (30 min). Consistent with the reaper, not isolated: a late cell's done could also have reclaimed 767; the run's trace (trajectory 94de3643) settles it when the computer is next awake. Design note for RES-17 and RES-13: with retention equal to the idle timeout, idle workers are frozen into the hibernate snapshot; a retention shorter than the idle timeout reaps them first.

- 12:10–12:15. The wake-orphan staging proof on test computer 71afbf14 (docs/problems/wake-during-deploy-vmctl-restart-orphans-the-vm-2026-10-22.md, steps 2–7).
  - **Attempt 1 (12:10).** The script's `computer start` lacked `--idempotency-key` and exited 2 with a usage error. No boot was in flight when FP0 restarted vmctl at 12:10:22. That bare restart reattached all four running VMs.
  - **Attempt 2 (12:13).** 71afbf14 was hibernated: the snapshot was not used (launch_config_mismatch), so a cold boot followed. vmctl restarted 5 s after admission. The graceful shutdown waited for the in-flight handler, and the boot published at 12:13:13 (epoch 13239), 10 s after admission. The new vmctl then reattached it.
  - **Attempt 3 (12:14).** A Node B journal trigger restarted vmctl 1 s after admission (12:14:29.1). The boot still published at 12:14:38 (epoch 13240), inside the 10 s graceful wait, and was reattached.
  - **Result.** Both runs exercised the guard (a start that publishes before the restart), not the in-flight close. The in-flight branch is unreachable by a graceful restart on today's staging, because a cold boot of this test computer (about 10 s) fits inside vmctl's 10 s graceful-shutdown wait. The 07:10 incident's boot took about 35 s. The in-flight close stays proven by the lane's tests, not on staging.
  - **What held.** The caller saw 502 "lifecycle resulting state was not observed" (step 4 expects that). The second start returned 200 at the published epoch, and the computer served. Negative control: each restart reattached all five managed VMs (route-authorized reattach adopted 5), including the owner's computer and the paper. There were no wedged lines and no lines saying a VM ran outside the registry.
  - **Residual (orange, small).** `/internal/vmctl/list` projects `ownershipResponse`, which carries no boot_claim, start_interruptions or failure_warmed_at. The plan's row checks (step 5, step 8) cannot be read on staging without SQL. A 12:15 poll of the row each second during a boot showed nothing, because those fields are not in the projection. The fix: add those fields to the list projection for operator visibility.

- 13:35. Owner ruling: "Yes, let's adopt these two rule changes". They amend the 00:20 rule of one live red per deploy, and observe first.
  1. **Two live reds per deploy, when disjoint.** A deploy may carry up to two live red batches when all of these hold: they touch different services or binaries; each has its own proof on its own test computer, run in parallel; each can be reverted alone (ideally a config flip); and neither touches the store schema, the boot path, auth/session or deploy routing. Those keep a deploy to themselves.
  2. **Observe first, made cheaper.** A new enforcing rule ships in observe everywhere, plus enforce on one selected test computer in the same deploy where a per-computer selector exists. A config-only flip whose enforce proof has passed on a test computer, and whose rollback is the same one-line flip, rides along as inert. Observe windows can be hours, not days, once the traffic that matters has passed through.
  Unchanged: the paper and the owner's computer are never the first place anything runs, and there is still one deploy at a time.
  FP0 also moved the P0 cuts ahead of SEC-14; FP3's plan has no dependency on SEC-14. The order: Q (FP4 round 3); R (the FP7 flip and SEC-06 in observe); S (FP3 cut 2: REC-02, SEC-11, Dolt stage 2; and ARCH-13); T (FP3 cut 3); U (SEC-14 deploy 1, alone).

- 2026-10-23 00:18-01:03 UTC: the agents VM (FP0's mirror and worker VM on node-a) was OOM-killed five times (23:49 to 00:40), losing each mirror chain. Cause, read from the per-VM cgroup memory.stat: cloud-hypervisor backs guest RAM with a memfd even under `--memory shared=off` and maps it private, so each written guest page costs a shmem page plus an anonymous copy (a 6 GiB guest held 5.9 GiB anon plus 6.1 GiB shmem). Owner: "shared memory for the VMs" and "It's safe to restart the VMs". A cloud-hypervisor wrapper in node-a's own infra config (outside this repo) now passes shared=on without mergeable=on (KSM was saving about 1.6 GB); deployed with rollback timers; every node-a VM restarted under it with anon 2-7 MiB; node-a available memory 11 to 18 GiB; no OOM since. The agents VM also gained /bin/bash and everyday tools (owner: "add that whenever"). Node B checked read-only: Firecracker maps guest RAM anonymous private (paper VMM RssShmem 0), so no double copy there.

- 01:5x Investigation (Sonnet, read-only, origin/main ef450642b): no live durability hole from segment-only receipts. Artifact GC never sweeps computer-event, computer-event-payload or pin-receipts. The tape sweep (operator-triggered only) keeps any digest named by committed Dolt append-receipt rows or by event bodies (payload refs, enforced by ValidateEventPins), reads segment-first then legacy, fails closed, and the reuse-branch mtime refresh is under casReuseMu with a 24 h grace floor. No timer deletes under platform-artifacts. The reuse branch is not a bug but leaves a segment depending on a legacy file; any legacy-delete tool (the repack) must use the chain-referenced digest set, never segment artifact records. Optional pin-side fix recorded as a residual: append the artifact record on a legacy hit when the computer's segment lacks it. Residual risk: a future event kind that pins a payload without an artifact ref.

Re-review only when a fix changes behavior on a tier 2 surface, one seat on the delta; nits never.

- 07:25 UTC: The self_development_operations replay divergence is found and reproduced (Sonnet; lane/fp0-selfdev-ops-replay 4f9e95a10 test, bc2d05986 and 6f1c83893 record). `selfdev/operations.go:524` RecordIntentError writes last_intent_error and updated_at by raw SQL with no tape event, and the projection has no last_intent_error column. It is called by rlm_reduce.go:1502 and :1543 (freeze and verify refusals, the clear after success) and by self_development_standing_consensus.go:70. The other raw writes are the unbound fallback behind `if s.projection != nil`. The test reproduces exactly t71's two differences, and the content_root difference has the same one cause (recomputed from the per-table hashes). Fix, red: the stamp goes on the tape. A live-only witness exclusion would change every computer's table hash and make older checkpoints unrestorable. FP0 decision (the conservative default): the stamp still sets updated_at, and both values go on the tape. Residuals: historic stamps need a one-time operator heal; Store.Reopen may swap in an ogStore without the mutation interceptor (code reading only), to be checked. The fix is with the same worker; tier 2 review after.

- 14:4x. Run-memory batching designed (lane/fp0-run-memory-batching 9082b6def, green): in the desk loop the tool-call row precedes the tools and the result precedes the next model call, so rows cannot share an event without a delay that changes what a planned restart recovers; a time buffer is refused (eleven readers derive state from durable rows: it would be a second state authority). Mechanism: the projector writes consecutive run-memory ops as one multi-row INSERT with no tape format change (old binaries replay op by op into the same rows), plus a group commit under runMemoryMu and grouped writes only where rows already exist together. Realistic saving -3% to -8% of the journal, not the -16% ceiling. Three deploys (counters, projector and group commit behind a setting, tool-loop groups), 500 product lines and 1,000 test lines, tier 2. FP0: build deploy 1 (counters) only after D17 and the HWM; park the rest until the counts are in. The design gets its architectural review when a build is chosen.

Failover triggers (toolloop.go:602, IsModelFailoverError :1179): gateway 502s (every provider failure and gateway deadline) after 3 in-gateway retries; a luna 429 or usage limit after the 5/20/60 s rate-limit waits, or at once once luna's breaker opens (5 failures, "circuit open"); sticky for the run, the next run starts on luna. The management task is a luna pin and gets no fallback. A Synthetic 429 after failover ends the run after its retries (about 2.5 min). Bunch 170 (Devin) and mirror lunapool-r1 started; the paper's policy file luna-synthetic-1023.toml is prepared (not committed). Found in passing (record owed): the gateway's streaming path writes upstream failures as an SSE line {"error": ...} that parseGatewaySSE ignores (it reads type:"error"); only the legacy Execute path streams, so failover is unaffected.

  - On main that shard takes 3 to 7 min; on PR #87, 6 min.
  - Cause: Server.done is unbuffered and Shutdown signals it with a non-blocking send. A Shutdown that arrives before Start reaches <-s.done is lost, and Start blocks forever.
  - Production carries the same path: SIGTERM -> Shutdown, so a service can outlive its own shutdown until the systemd stop timeout [H, not observed].
  - The record came first (lane/fp0-server-done-wakeup c5b58815d, docs/problems/server-shutdown-lost-wakeup-2026-10-23.md). - 03:0x. Lease seed timing investigation (a Sonnet worker, read-only, local measurement). seed_ms brackets more than the lease read: HeadsAligned, readLease, the cross-check window (the newest 2000 object headers and 500 choir_events rows), and appendLease("seed"), which is a full tape event append with several platform round trips. Local, warm, in process: 25 to 27 ms in all, of which the read is 0.4 ms, the append 16 ms and the 2000-row window 5 ms. The 50 ms target was a design number and was never measured. The gap on staging (T 220 ms; the paper 1.3 to 2.1 s) is a hypothesis: real round trips and fsyncs, cold journal reads, and waits on the engine and appender mutexes. Purely boot latency: allocations wait on the seeder, and the lease's correctness is unaffected. The next cheap step is per-phase timings on the seed log line (yellow). Changing the append is red and waits for those timings.

- 06:1x. The update R&D worker is done: docs/design/computer-update-architecture-rnd-2026-10-24.md (green; one divergent panel, six seats answered). Proposal: a floor the host owns and always patches (guest kernel and userspace, updater, capsule broker, boot wrapper), which never moves backward, not even on restore; and a lock the computer owns (base, ordered patch stack, app packages with pins). Effective source is the base plus the applied patches, a projection of the tape. Security updates rebuild the unchanged stack with the fixed toolchain, or replay it onto the fixed base, under a new evidence class that admits only deterministic transforms of host-signed fixes. First slice U1 (red, tier 2): make applies compound on one computer. New findings, not yet probed on staging: a base-changing deploy silently drops a computer's own Go change at boot; the layer binds to the whole guest-image digest, so a kernel-only patch drops every layer; a computer flagged divergent gets no updates at all, security included. Three owner questions for the design day. The PSA doc and the conductor's two problem docs land with it, docs only.
